## [2026-07-30 22:10:44] WARNING - Ignoring foreign parquet under OCCTO source pre…: /Users/qn5792/.codex/attachments/0dd0582b-4b27-4126-beca-13bf64a7e61f/pasted-text.txt

## My request for Codex:
Here we go again! We've already reached production but we still haven't stabliize the backfill. What the hell? Why are we having a hard time here vs the one we did with the other interconnector backfill dag? What makes OP capacity so different?

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:11:27.468Z

You’re right—Prod should not be discovering another live contract failure. I’m reading the complete tail now and will separate the immediate error from the deeper design reason this backfill became much harder than the original additive interconnector backfill. Don’t clear or rerun anything yet.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:12:27.700Z

This failure finally exposes the fundamental difference.

The original interconnector backfill was additive: history was missing, so it could safely add downloaded OCCTO data.

Operating Capacity is a surgical repair:

- Prod already has authoritative Available Capacity history.
- Operating Capacity is missing from those same source rows.
- Our backfill re-downloaded today’s version of the historical OCCTO files and replaced the complete rows.
- OCCTO has revised historical Available Capacity since Prod originally ingested it.

The safety guard caught a real change:

```text
2019-05-01 Chugoku/Kyushu reverse
Prod Available:      -925.1 MW
Current OCCTO file:  -911.2 MW
```

We promised to add Operating Capacity without changing Available Capacity, so it correctly aborted and rolled the 288 staged uploads back. The foreign-parquet warnings and forward-source drift were not the failure.

Why QA passed but Prod failed: QA’s existing history was apparently built from source versions sufficiently close to the newly downloaded OCCTO files. Prod contains older historical revisions, exposing the assumption that “re-downloading the same date means the other metrics are unchanged.” That assumption was wrong.

The correct design is now clear:

1. Use Prod’s existing source rows as the authoritative base.
2. Obtain Operating Capacity from the matching original archived OCCTO artifact if available; otherwise use the new download only as the Operating Capacity donor.
3. Join by exact business key.
4. Change only `operating_capacity_mw`.
5. Prove every other value—especially Available and minimum Available—remains identical before committing.
6. Reconcile and publish normally.

So Operating Capacity itself is not inherently complicated. We made it complicated by treating a column-level enrichment like a complete historical source replacement. Don’t clear this Prod task; the rollback completed. The next step is to determine whether the original archived OCCTO files contain Operating Capacity, because that would be the cleanest and most defensible donor.

### User - 2026-07-31T01:16:49.891Z

Tbh, as long as the "new and current" data comes from OCCTO (which they are), even the difference, I'd still prefer it as it's more current. It only makes sense to me to use the updated data, no? Instead of this checking which is which

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:17:18.186Z

I agree. If OCCTO’s current revised files are the authoritative source, then updating Available Capacity alongside Operating Capacity is more correct than preserving an older Prod value.

Our “Available must remain unchanged” rule was overly restrictive. It confused data integrity with value immutability. The real safety requirements should be:

- Everything comes from current OCCTO revised files.
- Complete 672-row daily coverage.
- Available, minimum Available, and Operating Capacity are populated consistently.
- Only the requested historical range changes.
- Future/outside-range data remains unchanged.
- Before/after differences are recorded for audit—not rejected.
- Publication and rollback checks remain intact.

This also matches the dashboard semantics nicely:

- **Latest** shows OCCTO’s latest revised historical truth.
- Historical **As-of** snapshots remain unchanged, preserving what was known at that time.

So the `-925.1 → -911.2 MW` change is not corruption; it is an OCCTO revision. We should remove the “Available unchanged inside repair range” blocker, retain a detailed change summary, and rerun the Prod backfill using the current OCCTO files. This makes SCR-1207 much closer to the original interconnector backfill and substantially simpler.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T09:22:27.105Z

Yes—safe to merge into `dev` specifically for UAT testing, but not yet safe to promote beyond `dev`.

The ordering matters:

1. Wait for Michael’s confirmation that the new Airflow image with `smp-common==0.6.0` is deployed.
2. Pause or disable automatic OCCTO TSDB publication temporarily.
3. Merge PR #21 into `dev`.
4. Confirm all DAGs import successfully.
5. Test the refactor first.
6. Test the backfill second.
7. Re-enable normal publication only after the refactor gates pass.

The backfill DAG is manual and defaults to `write=false`, so it will not start accidentally. The recurring refactored publisher is the part requiring the pre-merge pause/flag check.

### 1. Test the refactoring

Test forecast and actual publication separately against UAT:

- Run each with writes disabled.
- Confirm the common runtime reads the current TSDB values successfully.
- Inspect changed, unchanged, and missing counts.
- Confirm the expected 28 capacity and 7 actual series are represented.
- Enable a controlled write for genuinely new data.
- Confirm exact read-back verification succeeds.
- Rerun the same publication and require `no-change`.

This proves:

- the image contains `smp-common 0.6.0`;
- SDK configuration and credentials work;
- API/Athena latest-value reads work;
- Japan’s domain mapping still produces the correct payload;
- grouped writes and verification work against the real backend;
- idempotency works.

If the chosen data is already in UAT, `no-change` proves reading and comparison but does not exercise writing. We should deliberately find one safe missing UAT interval for the write test.

### 2. Test the backfill

Use progressive campaigns rather than immediately launching all 3.7 million points.

First, a one-day 35-series canary:

- Set both start dates and `end_date` to one completed date on or after `2025-04-01`.
- Run `write=false`.
- Expect:
  - capacity: 1,344 points;
  - actual flow: 2,016 points;
  - total: 3,360 points.
- Review source identities, gaps, counts, and would-write results.
- Run the identical campaign with `write=true`.
- Require exact read-back.
- Rerun with `write=false` and require `no-change`.

Then test chunk transitions with a bounded multi-day campaign. A 32-day period is useful because it crosses both chunk limits:

- capacity crosses its 31-day boundary;
- actual flow crosses several seven-day boundaries;
- logs should show all capacity chunks completing before any actual-flow chunk.

Finally:

- Run the full default campaign with `write=false`.
- Review missing source days and total would-write counts.
- Decide whether any `completed-with-warnings` gaps are acceptable.
- Run the full write.
- Rerun verify-only and require `no-change` for every complete chunk.

So: merge to `dev` after the image green signal and with recurring publication guarded. Treat that merge as the start of integration testing—not as proof that the change is production-ready.

## 1. Test refactored capacity publication

Run:

`japan_occto_capacity_reconciliation_dag`

Do not trigger `japan_occto_capacity_tsdb_publish_dag` directly—it requires handoff metadata produced by reconciliation.

### Verify-only

```json
{
  "tsdb_publish_enabled": true,
  "tsdb_endpoint": "uat",
  "tsdb_verify_only": true,
  "tsdb_full_consistency_audit": false
}
```

This reconciles capacity and triggers the refactored publisher without TSDB writes. Inspect the child `japan_occto_capacity_tsdb_publish_dag` result for `no-change` or `would-publish`.

### Controlled write

```json
{
  "tsdb_publish_enabled": true,
  "tsdb_endpoint": "uat",
  "tsdb_verify_only": false,
  "tsdb_full_consistency_audit": true
}
```

Require:

- publication status `published` or `no-change`;
- successful storage/read-back verification;
- successful consistency audit.

### Idempotency rerun

```json
{
  "tsdb_publish_enabled": true,
  "tsdb_endpoint": "uat",
  "tsdb_verify_only": true,
  "tsdb_full_consistency_audit": true
}
```

Require `no-change` and a successful audit.

## 2. Test refactored actual-flow publication

Run:

`japan_occto_actual_flow_completed_day_dag`

Use a known completed date for which source data is available.

### Verify-only

```json
{
  "target_date": "YYYY-MM-DD",
  "run_mode": "completed_day",
  "allow_progressive": false,
  "tsdb_publish_enabled": true,
  "tsdb_endpoint": "uat",
  "tsdb_verify_only": true
}
```

Prefer an already-published date first. It should return `no-change` and pass the consistency audit.

Nuance: if the selected date genuinely needs changes, the publication step should return `would-publish`, but the subsequent consistency audit may fail because verify-only intentionally did not write those changes. That failure would represent the expected mismatch, not necessarily a publisher failure.

### Controlled write

Use a safe completed date that is missing or outdated in UAT:

```json
{
  "target_date": "YYYY-MM-DD",
  "run_mode": "completed_day",
  "allow_progressive": false,
  "tsdb_publish_enabled": true,
  "tsdb_endpoint": "uat",
  "tsdb_verify_only": false
}
```

Require `published`, exact read-back, and a passing consistency audit.

Then rerun the same date with `tsdb_verify_only: true` and require `no-change`.

Do not use `japan_occto_actual_flow_dag`; TSDB publication is intentionally limited to completed-day runs.

## 3. Test the backfill: one-day canary

Run:

`japan_occto_tsdb_backfill_dag`

Choose one known source-complete date on or after `2025-04-01`.

### Preview

```json
{
  "capacity_start_date": "2025-04-01",

  "end_date": "2025-04-01",

}
```

- Capacity: 1,344 points
- Actual flow: 2,016 points
- Total: 3,360 points

Review source identities, gaps, `would_write_row_count`, and unchanged counts.

### Write

```json
{
  "capacity_start_date": "2025-04-01",
  "actual_flow_start_date": "2025-04-01",
  "end_date": "2025-04-01",
  "write": true
}
```

Require successful publication, exact read-back, and independent audit for all complete chunks.

### Confirm idempotency

Rerun the preview configuration with `write: false`. Require `no-change`.

## 4. Full backfill preview

```json
{
  "capacity_start_date": "2021-01-01",
  "actual_flow_start_date": "2025-04-01",
  "end_date": null,
  "write": false
}
```

`end_date: null` resolves to the latest completed JST day using the Airflow run’s logical date.

Before writing, review:

- resolved end date;
- capacity and actual source gaps;
- complete versus incomplete chunks;
- total would-write rows;
- whether `completed-with-warnings` is operationally acceptable.

## 5. Full backfill write

Only after accepting the preview:

```json

  "capacity_start_date": "2021-01-01",
  "actual_flow_start_date": "2025-04-01",
  "end_date": null,
  "write": true
}
```

Then run the same campaign again with `write: false` and require `no-change` for every source-complete chunk.

So the core DAGs are:

- Refactored capacity: `japan_occto_capacity_reconciliation_dag`
- Refactored actual flow: `japan_occto_actual_flow_completed_day_dag`
- Historical campaign: `japan_occto_tsdb_backfill_dag`

`occto_tsdb_backfill/campaign-report.json`

1. The common publisher wrote and performed exact TSDB read-back.
2. The independent Japan audit rebuilt the expected data separately from parquet and compared it with TSDB.

For every completed chunk, check:

```text
publication.status = published or no-change
audit.status = matched
audit.mismatch_count = 0
audit.expected_hash = audit.actual_hash
```

The DAG would have failed if an independent audit had found a mismatch. So the green run is more meaningful than just “the API accepted our writes.”

## 3. Local actual-flow spot probe

From `smp-japan`, this performs a read-only UAT query using interactive Okta authentication:

```bash
direnv exec . uv run --no-sync python \
  scripts/probe_occto_tsdb_actual_flow.py \
  --target-date 2025-04-01 \
  --endpoint uat \
  --user-auth
```

Expected for the selected actual-flow series:

```text
status: complete
expected_point_count: 288
observed_point_count: 288
missing_point_count: 0
unexpected_point_count: 0
duplicate_row_count: 0
null_value_count: 0
writes_attempted: false
```

You can provide `--series-id <id>` to spot-check specific catalogue series. Without it, the script checks its default POC interconnector.

That probe is useful psychological proof that “yes, the values exist in this reality,” but it only checks one actual-flow series/day at a time. The exact read-only backfill rerun is the exhaustive confirmation across all 35 series and the whole requested history.

So my recommended sequence is:

1. Inspect the successful campaign report.
2. Rerun the exact range with `write=false`.
3. Require zero would-write rows.
4. Use the local probe on a few dates—start, middle, and latest—as an independent spot check.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

## SCR-1218 Spike Closeout

**TL;DR: GO.** SMP India can read and decrypt the Khaba Generation source file from Mumbai in both dev and production.

### What we changed

- Added a manual, read-only Airflow probe DAG.
- Verified the workload identity and CDH role-assumption chain.
- Tested exact-object metadata, download, checksum, KMS decryption, network path, and temporary-file cleanup.
- Updated `smp-common` to `0.6.0` and aligned the package index.
- Fixed India DAG git-sync configuration checks.
- Removed unnecessary bucket-listing requirements and suppressed credential-bearing AWS DEBUG logs.
- Promoted everything through dev, QA, and prod.

### CDH configuration

The initial role assumption worked, but S3 returned `403`. Adding `khaba_generation` to the India CDH projects provisioned the missing dataset permissions.

### Evidence

Both dev and prod successfully downloaded:

`KHABA/FTP_DATA29-07-2026.csv`

- Region: `ap-south-1`
- Size: `2,172,772` bytes
- SHA-256: `98dc88eb1f9ebcd10d4195e31bd60ddb050692f6030739fd9d71f584c0474618`
- Encryption: SSE-KMS
- KMS decryption: proven
- Dev cross-account path: proven
- Production path: proven

### Decision

Proceed with SCR-1209. If ingestion needs dynamic file discovery rather than a calculated key, validate prefix-listing permission during implementation.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T08:58:29.874Z

You’re right to hesitate. The distinction is operational, not just code style.

A single DAG is less UI clutter, but the 05:00 run becomes a special snowflake: it processes two dates, couples realtime and reconciliation failures, and makes alerts/backfills harder to interpret.

Two DAGs give us:

- `khaba_generation_realtime`: every 15 minutes, current IST day, low-latency behavior.
- `khaba_generation_reconciliation`: 05:00 IST, previous day, completeness/correction behavior; manual `target_date` override doubles as backfill.

Both call the same parser/publisher module, so there’s very little duplicated code. The cost is one extra DAG definition and two test files. I’d choose **two DAGs** here—SRP actually buys us cleaner retries, alerts, and ownership rather than ceremony for ceremony’s sake.

We still need to lock the time and failure contracts.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T09:24:15.235Z

<proposed_plan>
# SCR-1209 — Khaba Generation to TSDB

## Summary

Implement two India Airflow DAGs sharing one ingestion module:

- **Realtime:** every 15 minutes, process the current IST day.
- **Reconciliation/backfill:** 05:00 IST for the previous day; manual runs accept a target date.

Each run reads the exact daily S3 object, calculates completed 15-minute averages, and publishes only new or corrected TSDB points.

## Implementation

- Read `s3://cdh-indiaassetsgeneration-337381/KHABA/FTP_DATA%d-%m-%Y.csv` through the environment’s CDH project role in `ap-south-1`.
- Publish four inferred fields:
  - `Active Power-avg MFM-OUT(Meter Power)`
  - `Module SurfaceTemperature`
  - `AmbientTemperature`
  - `GHI_W`
- Parse one-minute readings in `Asia/Kolkata`; label each average by interval start and convert timestamps to UTC for TSDB.
- Preserve negative active-power values and publish source values without unit conversion.
- Ignore the malformed inverter-column tail by reading only the four required fields.
- Realtime runs:
  - Reprocess all completed intervals for the current day.
  - Ignore only the trailing incomplete interval.
  - Fail before publishing on missing files, internal gaps, duplicates, wrong dates, or invalid values.
- Reconciliation requires all 1,440 minutes/96 intervals; incomplete days fail without partial publication.
- Use `smp-common` publication handling so unchanged points are skipped, corrections are updated, and written values are verified.

## Interfaces and Scheduling

- DAGs:
  - `india_khaba_generation_realtime`: every 15 minutes IST.
  - `india_khaba_generation_reconciliation`: daily at 05:00 IST.
- Reconciliation exposes an optional ISO `target_date`; scheduled runs default to the previous IST date.
- Add environment-specific JSON variable `KHABA_GENERATION_TSDB_SERIES_IDS` with keys:
  - `active_power`
  - `module_surface_temperature`
  - `ambient_temperature`
  - `ghi`
- Dev/QA publish to TSDB UAT; prod publishes to TSDB prod.
- Keep both DAGs paused upon creation until the four IDs are supplied. Missing mappings produce a clear failure.
- Log only object, interval, row-count, and publication summaries; never credentials or temporary tokens.

## Tests and Rollout

- Test exact S3 key/date construction, role assumption parameters, timezone conversion, four-field parsing, 15-minute averages, negative values, partial intervals, gaps, duplicates, malformed values, and full-day reconciliation.
- Verify unchanged points are skipped, corrected points are republished, and TSDB readback succeeds.
- Use a small sanitized fixture reflecting the supplied CSV, including its broken trailing headers.
- Commit and promote the SCR-1218 probe cleanup separately first.
- Implement and validate parsing while TSDB IDs are pending.
- Once Mateo supplies the entries, confirm their names/units match the four inferred fields, configure IDs, test manually in dev, publish to UAT from QA, then promote and unpause prod.
- Observe the first realtime cycle and following 05:00 reconciliation before considering [SCR-1209](https://tractebel-et.atlassian.net/browse/SCR-1209) complete.

## Assumptions

- “Two topology values” was a transcription error for the two temperature fields; the four-column mapping above is locked pending confirmation from the TSDB entry names.
- GHI means `GHI_W`, not `POA`.
- The source file is refreshed every 15 minutes but contains one-minute measurements.
- No schema or unit conversion is introduced unless Mateo’s TSDB metadata contradicts the source values.
</proposed_plan>

We can test almost everything except the final TSDB interaction.

Right now, triggering the realtime DAG without IDs will:

1. Assume the CDH role.
2. Download the live S3 file.
3. Parse and validate it.
4. Build the 15-minute averages.
5. Then fail at `Missing Airflow Variable KHABA_GENERATION_TSDB_SERIES_IDS`.

So reaching that specific error already proves the source side worked—but an intentionally failed DAG is a bit janky.

I recommend adding a `validate_only` manual-run parameter. When enabled, it would stop before resolving IDs and return a clean summary:

- S3 object/key accessed
- source row count
- completed 15-minute intervals
- four detected fields
- first/last timestamps
- excluded partial interval
- validation status

Then we can:

- Run realtime validation against today’s live file.
- Run reconciliation validation against a completed historical date and expect exactly 1,440 rows / 96 intervals.
- Continue testing TSDB payload construction locally using fake IDs, as the existing tests already do.

What remains impossible without the real IDs:

- Confirming series metadata and units.
- TSDB authentication against those series.
- Actual writes, correction handling, and readback verification.

So yeah: add `validate_only`, test both DAGs live in dev, and we’ll have strong evidence without fake TSDB writes.

Recommended correction:

- Accept a missing trailing tail.
- Continue rejecting internal gaps, duplicates, malformed rows, or wrong dates.
- Reconcile every complete 15-minute interval available.
- Ignore the final partial interval.
- Report `day_complete`, source row count, last timestamp, and missing-tail count instead of failing.
- Also replace the deprecated Airflow Variable import.

That preserves safety while matching the actual CDH file behavior.

### User - 2026-07-31T10:28:45.575Z

wait, I don't get it. why did we assume wrong? What's the point of recon anyway?

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T10:29:07.463Z

We assumed wrong because we had only inspected a **current-day** file. It contained one reading per minute, so we extrapolated that a finalized previous-day file would contain:

`24 hours × 60 minutes = 1,440 rows`

That was reasonable, but unproven. July 30 is our first finalized-file evidence, and it contains a contiguous sequence starting at midnight but fewer than 1,440 rows. We don’t yet know whether that’s due to delivery lag, a cutoff before midnight, or missing final data.

The reconciliation DAG exists to catch what realtime may miss:

- Realtime repeatedly processes today’s evolving file.
- At midnight, realtime switches to the new day.
- Reconciliation rereads yesterday’s supposedly final file at 05:00.
- It publishes late additions or corrections; unchanged TSDB points are skipped.
- Its manual date also provides backfilling.

So reconciliation is still useful. In fact, this failure is doing its job: it exposed that the “final” file isn’t shaped as expected.

I wouldn’t loosen the rule blindly yet. First we should make validation mode report July 30’s exact row count and final timestamp without failing. Then we can decide whether the missing tail is normal source behavior or an actual CDH delivery problem.

### User - 2026-07-31T10:29:27.597Z

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T10:29:40.042Z

Next move: gather evidence before changing reconciliation policy.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Baraluga Mind Agent Instructions

You are maintaining a local markdown second brain.

Your job is to turn raw captures into durable, useful wiki pages without inventing facts.

## Core Rules

- Read any nested `AGENTS.md` files that apply to the files being processed or edited.
- Preserve source material during ingest.
- Do not delete raw inputs unless explicitly asked.
- After processing an `inbox/` file, move it to the appropriate `sources/` folder when it remains useful as evidence.
- Leave an `inbox/` file in place when it still needs follow-up, review, or clarification.
- Do not fabricate details, links, names, dates, or decisions.
- Distinguish facts from inferences.
- Prefer small updates to existing wiki pages over creating duplicate notes.
- Link related wiki pages using Obsidian-style links like `[[concept-name]]`.
- Keep prose direct, dense, and useful.
- Use `UNCERTAIN:` when a claim needs verification.
- If source material is ambiguous, say so in the page.

## Actions

`actions.md` is the canonical register for follow-up work.

- Put every captured follow-up, task, owner commitment, or call to action in `actions.md`.
- Keep `actions.md` organized into exactly these top-level sections: `Today`, `Open`, `Waiting`, and `Done`.
- Add newly captured actions to `Open` by default.
- Use `Today` only when the user explicitly selects or prioritizes work for today.
- Use `Waiting` for actions blocked on another person, external answer, access, or event.
- Do not bury active TODOs inside wiki pages.
- Wiki pages may preserve context for an action, but the actionable checkbox belongs in `actions.md`.
- When adding an action, include a `Context` link to the related wiki page and a `Source` path to the captured evidence.
- Do not duplicate an existing open action; update it only when the new source adds material context.
- Move completed actions to `Done` instead of deleting them.
- Reconcile existing actions against new evidence during ingest. Move completed, superseded, or no-longer-relevant actions to `Done` with a brief source-backed reason; do not infer closure from ambiguous evidence.
- Source summaries may include non-canonical `Next Steps` when reflecting meeting content, but active tracking still belongs in `actions.md`.

## File Naming

Use lowercase kebab-case for all new files and directories.

Good:

- `wiki/concepts/second-brain.md`
- `wiki/projects/baraluga-mind.md`
- `wiki/decisions/2026-07-04-use-markdown-wiki.md`
- `inbox/2026-07-04-team-sync.md`

Avoid:

- `Second Brain.md`
- `second_brain.md`
- `Team Sync.md`
- `2026_07_04_notes.md`

## Process Improvement

Do not edit `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, or nested instruction files during normal ingest unless explicitly asked.

If you notice repeated friction, ambiguity, bad output, a useful convention, or instruction bloat, append a short note to `process-notes.md` instead.

Use this format:

```markdown
### YYYY-MM-DD

- Observation: ...
- Suggested rule: ...
- Example: ...
```

Instruction files should stay small and scoped. Prefer one local, source-specific instruction over expanding the root file when a rule applies only to one folder or content type.

## Ingest Behavior

When processing files from `inbox/` or `sources/`:

1. Identify the source type: article, meeting, voice note, PDF, pasted note, or mixed capture.
2. Extract durable entities:
   - people
   - projects
   - concepts
   - decisions
   - open questions
   - action items
3. Update existing pages when possible.
4. Create new pages only when the topic is likely to matter again.
5. Add or update captured action items in `actions.md`.
6. Add a short `Sources` section with the source filename or path.
7. Move processed `inbox/` files to the appropriate `sources/` subfolder when they remain useful as evidence:
   - `sources/articles/` for articles, links, and reading notes
   - `sources/meetings/` for meeting transcripts, agendas, and summaries
   - `sources/voice/` for voice-note transcripts and dictated thoughts
   - `sources/pdfs/` for PDFs and extracted PDF notes
   - `sources/notes/` for manual or AI-maintained daily notes
   - Leave the current Asia/Manila day's live daily note in `inbox/` unless the user explicitly asks to close or ingest it.
   - For a live `inbox/YYYY-MM-DD.md` daily note, reconcile its provisional task states with `actions.md`, then preserve it in `sources/notes/`.
   - When a daily note duplicates content in a Codex conversation export, prefer the structured daily note and use the transcript as backup evidence.
8. Add a short `Last Updated` date using `YYYY-MM-DD`.
9. Validate the complete ingest diff, then commit and push all ingest changes using an unscoped conventional commit.

## Wiki Page Shape

Use this structure when creating a new durable page:

```markdown
# Page Title

## Summary

One to three paragraphs explaining the durable point of the page.

## Details

Concrete facts, context, links, tradeoffs, and examples.

## Open Questions

- UNCERTAIN: ...

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

If a section is not useful, omit it.

## Decision Page Shape

Use `wiki/decisions/YYYY-MM-DD-short-decision.md`.

```markdown
# Decision: Short Decision

## Decision

State the decision plainly.

## Context

Why this came up.

## Rationale

Why this option was chosen.

## Tradeoffs

What this makes easier or harder.

## Sources

- `sources/...`

Last Updated: YYYY-MM-DD
```

## Review Output

After an ingest pass, report:

- pages created
- pages updated
- actions added or updated in `actions.md`
- source files processed
- uncertain claims
- new or suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, or jargon needing confirmation
- suggested next cleanup

Also include a consolidated handover section titled `Needs Confirmation`.

`Needs Confirmation` should be pleasant to scan and useful as a one-by-one clarification queue for the user. It should include only:

- all current needs-confirmation items from the ingest review, including suspicious names, acronyms, systems, projects, jargon, ambiguous owners, and uncertain high-impact claims.

Do not include the full `Open` action register in this handover by default. `actions.md` remains the canonical action register, but many captured TODOs may belong to other people or teams and should not be presented as Brian's assignment unless the user explicitly asks.

Format `Needs Confirmation` as grouped checklists by durable area when possible, such as `Team Operations`, `SMP Platform`, `Japan Interconnector`, and `Alerting and Ops`.

For each item, include:

- the exact term or claim to confirm;
- why confirmation is needed;
- the source path or related wiki page.

Keep `Needs Confirmation` concise enough to work through manually. Exclude items that are merely low-priority, already resolved, or not currently worth asking the user about.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.055Z

## Conclusion

The highlighted Tohoku → Tokyo zero is **not a scraping or dashboard calculation error**. It comes directly from OCCTO and represents **remaining available capacity (空容量)**, not total transfer capability or physical flow.

At the screenshot’s `2026-07-22 15:00 JST` point:

| OCCTO value | MW |
|---|---:|
| Operating capacity | 5,250 |
| Planned flow | 5,090 |
| Margin | 160 |
| Available capacity | **0** |
| Actual flow, six 5-minute readings averaged | **5,083** |

This reconciles exactly:

`available capacity = operating capacity − margin − planned flow`

`5,250 − 160 − 5,090 = 0`

OCCTO officially defines available capacity this way, while actual flow is separately measured physical flow. Therefore, substantial actual flow alongside zero remaining capacity is expected. [OCCTO’s interconnector usage explanation](https://www.occto.or.jp/houkokusho/2025/files/denryokujukyuu_2024_250910.pdf)

## Checks completed

- Live OCCTO revised `D` data returned the exact Grafana values:
  - forward `0`
  - reverse `−7,374.4`
  - actual-flow average `5,083`
- Interval alignment is correct: OCCTO’s `15:30` end label is stored as the `15:00–15:30` interval.
- Tohoku/Tokyo maps correctly to `相馬双葉幹線` in [mappings.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-japan/dags/src/scripts/occto_capacity/mappings.py:23).
- The scraper reads OCCTO’s `akyury1` available-capacity field directly in [daily.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-japan/dags/src/scripts/occto_capacity/daily.py:294).
- Grafana selects the correct interconnector, direction, capacity field, and 30-minute actual-flow average in [japan_interconnector_with_lookback.json](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-dashboard/dashboards/japan/japan_interconnector_with_lookback.json:838).
- Historical live samples also showed many source-side zeroes: 15–20 of 48 half-hours on June 1, June 15, July 1, and July 15.
- 60 focused scraper/reconciliation tests and 23 dashboard tests passed.

## Genuine issues worth noting

There previously was a historical-retention bug where completed daily capacity could fall back to weekly data. The completed-day preservation fix is now on the production branch, with the behavior documented in [occto-capacity-reconciliation.md](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-japan/docs/occto-capacity-reconciliation.md:22). It does **not** explain the July 22 zero, which exactly matches OCCTO.

A separate latent risk remains: revised duplicate actual-flow parquet files can be averaged together because Grafana does not deduplicate revisions. That is documented in [occto-actual-flow-backfill-probe.md](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-japan/docs/occto-actual-flow-backfill-probe.md:108), but it did not affect this example—the displayed `5,083` exactly matches OCCTO’s final six readings.

The most useful improvement would be to rename the chart series to **“Remaining Available Capacity (空容量)”** and explain the formula in the panel description. The current word “Available” makes the perfectly valid zero-versus-flow relationship look suspicious. No repository files were changed.

What they confirm:

- Hiromi specified OCCTO as the capacity source.
- The deck uses the same daily `D / D+1 / D+2` screen and CSV workflow we investigated.
- The CSV schema shown in the deck matches our downloaded artifact exactly.
- Available capacity and actual flow were intended as separate series.

But slide 17 labels OCCTO’s `運用容量`—operating capacity—as “Availability.” That is not the field Grafana currently plots. Grafana plots `空容量`, meaning remaining/spare capacity.

That explains everything:

```text
Operating capacity: 5,250 MW
Planned flow:        5,090 MW
Margin:                160 MW
Remaining capacity:      0 MW
Actual flow:          5,083 MW
```

So the scraper is faithfully retrieving OCCTO data, but the epic may have intended a different OCCTO field for the delivered period. This is probably a field-definition mismatch, not bad scraping.

Suggested reply:

> Hi USHIO-san — thanks for flagging this. We traced the Tohoku/Tokyo example back to the raw OCCTO data and found the reason for the zero.
>
> Grafana currently shows OCCTO’s `空容量`, which is the remaining/spare capacity after planned flow and margin are deducted. For 22 July at 15:00, OCCTO reports 5,250 MW operating capacity, 5,090 MW planned flow and 160 MW margin, so the remaining capacity is correctly calculated as zero. The actual flow was 5,083 MW, which is why the green line can still be high while the blue line is zero.
>

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.062Z

This is a solid **M-sized change: roughly 5–8 engineering days**, plus deployment/backfill runtime. Not horrible, but definitely not “add two Grafana lines and call it Tuesday.”

Good news: OCCTO already publishes `運用容量` in yearly, monthly, weekly, and daily data. No new source or scraper mechanism is needed.

The work exists because we deliberately didn’t persist it:

- Add operating capacity to the source model and all four parsers.
- Extend raw and effective Parquet schemas.
- Carry it through reconciliation using the same yearly → monthly → weekly → daily precedence.
- Include it in current and future look-back snapshots.
- Refresh CDH schemas across environments.
- Add forward/reverse operating-capacity lines to all seven Grafana panels.
- Extend validation and regression tests.
- Re-extract historical daily data because existing Parquet contains only available capacity.

### Backfill reality

OCCTO serves daily capacity back to 2016, so backfilling final revised operating capacity from FY2019 onward is feasible. That’s approximately 1.8 million normalized rows—material, but perfectly manageable with our existing chunked backfill machinery.

The annoying caveat: we **cannot faithfully retrofit operating capacity into old “as-of” snapshots**. Those historical snapshots only retained available capacity, and OCCTO no longer exposes every old publication vintage.

We can provide:

- historical final/revised operating capacity for delivered dates from FY2019 onward;
- operating capacity in every new look-back snapshot after rollout.

For existing historical snapshot selections, the new line would remain unavailable. We shouldn’t silently substitute today’s final revision because that would undermine the point of look-back.

### Rough breakdown

| Work | Estimate |
|---|---:|
| Extraction, models and schemas | 1–2 days |
| Reconciliation and snapshot handling | 1–2 days |
| Backfill tooling, execution and validation | 1–2 days |
| CDH and seven Grafana panels | 1 day |
| QA, deployment and production verification | 1 day |

My working estimate would be **6 days**, with **8 days as the safe commitment**.

If they also want operating capacity published through the new TSDB catalogue, add another **1–2 days plus catalogue approval**, since that creates 14 additional directional series.

Recommended scope: do the full Grafana/CDH implementation and final-history backfill now, document the historical snapshot boundary clearly, and let all future look-back snapshots accumulate correctly.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.067Z

Yes — we should extend the existing capacity pipeline. Definitely not one DAG per metric.

The OCCTO response already contains Operating Capacity alongside Available Capacity:

- Daily JSON: `akyury1` = Available Capacity, `unyr1` = Operating Capacity.
- Yearly/monthly/weekly downloads also already contain `運用容量`.
- No additional request, endpoint, schedule, or authentication is needed.

The current parser simply discards Operating Capacity. For example, [daily.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-japan/dags/src/scripts/occto_capacity/daily.py:330) reads `akyury1` but ignores `unyr1`.

Recommended shape:

```text
Existing capacity DAG
  └── existing OCCTO response
        ├── available_capacity_mw
        └── operating_capacity_mw  ← retain this too
```

DAGs should represent a source/publication cadence, not individual metrics. A separate Operating Capacity DAG would make the same OCCTO request twice and risk the two metrics reflecting different revisions.

Change surface

| Area | Expected files |
|---|---:|
| Four source parsers: yearly/monthly/weekly/daily | 4 |
| Shared record and parquet schema | 2 |
| Reconciliation output and validation | 3 |
| Historical backfill parser | 1 |
| Grafana dashboard | 1 |
| CDH catalogue/schema samples | 4–8 |
| Tests | ~8–12 |

So roughly **20–25 changed files**, but only around **10 production Python files** contain meaningful logic changes. The four Airflow DAG definition files themselves should need **zero or near-zero changes**.

The implementation is basically adding one column end-to-end:

- `operating_capacity_mw` in the normalized source dataset.
- `effective_operating_capacity_mw` in the reconciled dataset.
- Carry it from the same winning yearly/monthly/weekly/daily record as Available Capacity.
- Add forward and reverse Operating Capacity lines to all seven Grafana panels.
- Update the existing backfill to read the `運用容量` column it currently ignores.

The code itself is probably **2–3 days**. Schema rollout, backfill, validation, and environment promotion are what keep the safe overall estimate at **5–8 days**. The backfill is not a new scraper either—we extend the existing capacity backfill and rerun it.

### User - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.067Z

confident on this? If u wanna do any probing shit, by all means pls, we wanna close-to-one-shot this, if possible lol.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.068Z

After probing it properly: yes, I’m highly confident—call it **95% on the implementation design**.

What I verified live today:

| OCCTO source | Rows returned | Operating Capacity |
|---|---:|---|
| Daily | 672 | `unyr1` present |
| Weekly | 4,704 | `unyr1` present |
| Monthly | 1,708 | `unyr1` present |
| Yearly | 9,380 | `unyr1` present on every row |

I also checked the five original downloadable OCCTO CSVs: **36,912 rows total, zero missing `運用容量` values**.

So the conclusion is firm:

- No new endpoint.
- No additional request configuration.
- No new authentication.
- No new schedules.
- No new DAGs.
- No separate Operating Capacity scraper.

We should extend the existing four capacity extractors to retain `unyr1`, then carry it through the same reconciliation winner as Available Capacity. That guarantees both values come from the same OCCTO publication and revision.

The one implementation trap

Existing parquet files don’t contain the new column. If we simply make it mandatory, reconciliation will reject old files during rollout.

I tested the safe migration path: read old parquet using the evolved schema and inject `NULL` for the missing column. That works. Therefore we should:

1. Add nullable `operating_capacity_mw`.
2. Make the reader tolerate old parquet without that column.
3. Refresh/backfill the existing capacity data.
4. Validate Operating Capacity is populated.
5. Refresh CDH, then expose the Grafana lines.

This gives us a rolling migration without breaking Available Capacity.

Refined change size:

- About **11 production Python files**.
- **Zero DAG definition files** expected.
- One Grafana dashboard.
- CDH catalogue plus three schema samples.
- Roughly 8–12 test files.
- Around **25–30 touched files total**, although much of that is tests and generated schema/config.

The original **5–8 day estimate still holds**. The extraction change is genuinely simple; the time is in safely migrating the schema, performing the backfill, and validating the result across dev/QA/prod.

Strong recommendation: **extend the capacity pipeline; do not create one DAG per metric.** That would duplicate the same request and create revision-alignment problems for no benefit.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.073Z

Operating Capacity is **one value per direction per interval**—it does not have min/max variants.

- Operating Capacity: one value × two directions = two lines.

### User - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.073Z

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.073Z

We’re good. I’d keep the same design language and avoid introducing unrelated colours.

Direction stays encoded by colour family; metric type is encoded by shade and line style:

| Metric | Forward | Reverse | Style |
|---|---|---|---|
| Available | Blue `#4472C4` | Orange `#ED7D31` | Solid, 2px |
| Min Available | Light blue `#8FAADC` | Light orange `#F4B183` | Long dash, 1px |
| Operating Capacity | Dark blue `#1F4E79` | Dark orange `#C65911` | Short dotted, 1–2px |
| Actual Flow | Green | — | Existing solid |
| DA Spread | Purple | — | Existing right axis |

So blue always means forward and orange always means reverse. Users distinguish Available, Minimum, and Operating by intensity and stroke pattern.

Both Operating Capacity lines should be visible by default and toggleable through the legend. This approach is already reflected in the SCR-1206 plan.

### User - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.082Z

I already ran CDH registration on all envs earlier lol.

Question... how will this reconcile the already backfilled data in QA? Like, if we take a look at QA, all data before "now" is actually backfilled with daily capacity... if we rerun weekly, monthly, and yearly, will they overwrite? Or only forward data?

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.084Z

The current effective yearly parquet is regenerated and replaced as a whole, but the backfilled daily records remain the selected values.

Why:

- Reconciliation always includes the immutable daily backfill source.
- Priority is `daily > weekly > monthly > yearly`.
- Therefore weekly/monthly/yearly cannot displace a backfilled daily interval.

So rerunning them affects:

- Forward periods beyond the daily window.
- Any genuine gaps in the historical daily backfill.
- Not complete backfilled daily intervals.

One important detail: reconciliation selects a whole winning record; it does not mix metrics between granularities. Because old backfilled daily records lack Operating Capacity, their historical Operating Capacity stays null. Weekly/monthly/yearly will not fill it underneath the daily winner—that is what SCR-1207 will address.

Also, existing `historical` lookback snapshots are not overwritten. Only the current `all/part-current-YYYY.parquet` files are republished; historical snapshots remain immutable.

So yes, it is safe to rerun all three in QA. If the daily backfill is complete, the practical result is “populate Operating Capacity forward from now” while preserving past Available Capacity exactly.

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.086Z

You’re understanding it correctly. Operating Capacity should behave like Available Capacity in `Latest`; the moving start is a real data gap, not a Grafana issue.

Root cause:

- The completed-day DAG preserves Available Capacity from OCCTO’s CSV.
- That same CSV already contains `運用容量`.
- But our shared historical/completed-day parser currently sets:

```python
operating_capacity_mw=None
```

- Daily records outrank weekly/monthly/yearly during reconciliation.
- Therefore, as each date becomes “completed,” its daily record wins with Operating Capacity = null. The visible Operating Capacity boundary consequently moves forward every day.

This is precisely what SCR-1207 was intended to repair.

The fix has two parts:

1. Parse `運用容量` in the shared completed-day/backfill parser so newly completed dates retain Operating Capacity.
2. Backfill existing historical daily files and reconcile again so `Latest` gains continuous historical Operating Capacity.

One important lookback rule:

- `Latest` will be repaired historically.
- Future `As of` snapshots created after the repair will contain that history.
- Existing snapshots such as `2026-07-25` remain unchanged because they represent what the pipeline actually held on July 25. Rewriting them would falsify the lookback record.

So yes: same practical behaviour as Available Capacity in `Latest`, while historical `As of` snapshots remain immutable. I’d implement SCR-1207 next—the screenshots have confirmed both the need and the exact failure mode.

---
name: delegate-main-task
description: Delegate work to a separate, sidebar-visible Codex task in the same project, return immediately, and have that task message its result back to the origin. Use only when the user explicitly asks for a new, main, peer, or separate Codex task that reports back, or explicitly invokes $delegate-main-task. Do not use for ordinary subagent requests, unsolicited delegation, monitoring an existing task, or forking.
---

# Delegate Main Task

Create a normal peer task in the origin's current project. Let the origin finish its dispatch turn
immediately; have the peer start a new origin turn when its result is ready.

## Rules

- Treat `$delegate-main-task` or an explicit request for a new/separate Codex task as
  authorization to create it.
- Use Codex task tools, loading them with tool search when necessary.
- Require both `create_thread` and `send_message_to_thread` to be callable. If either is
  unavailable, explain that event-based delegation is unavailable and do not imitate it with
  polling.
- Use `create_thread`; never use `fork_thread`, subagent tools, worktrees, or cloud tasks.
- Create the task in the same saved project as the origin with
  `target.environment.type: local`.
- If the origin is projectless, create a projectless task.
- Preserve the user's scope, permissions, constraints, and completion criteria.
- Do not set a model or reasoning effort unless the user explicitly requests it.
- Do not archive the delegated task.

## Delegate

1. Resolve the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId` from app context or `list_threads`.
   Identify its project and use `list_projects` to resolve the exact `projectId`. Never guess
   between plausible tasks or projects.
2. Generate a short unique delegation ID.
3. Write a self-contained worker prompt containing:
   - The requested work, relevant context and paths, constraints, and expected evidence.
   - The delegation ID plus the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId`.
   - An instruction to call `send_message_to_thread` on the origin when the work reaches a
     terminal state. Send a concise, self-contained message containing the delegation ID,
     `completed`, `blocked`, or `failed`, the result, artifact paths or links, and the recommended
     next action.
   - An instruction to send `needs_input` with the exact question as soon as new user input or
     authority is required.
   - An instruction to keep ordinary progress in the worker task and send exactly one terminal
     result message to the origin.
4. Call `create_thread` once:
   - Saved project:
     `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`
   - Projectless origin: `target: {type: "projectless"}`
5. Retain the returned `threadId` and `hostId`.
6. End the origin's dispatch turn immediately. Tell the user the task is running and will message
   this task when ready. Do not call `wait_threads` or repeatedly read the worker.
7. Emit `::created-thread{threadId="THREAD_ID"}` on its own line in the origin's final response.

## Handle Returned Messages

- Treat a message carrying the delegation ID as a result from the existing delegation, not as a
  request to create another task.
- On `needs_input`, surface the exact question. After the user answers, send the answer to the
  worker with `send_message_to_thread`; then finish the origin turn again.
- On `completed`, integrate the returned result and continue the parent objective if work remains.
- Report failures or incomplete work plainly.

</skill>

### Step 2 — Find the historical repair boundary

Airflow QA:

Grafana Explore:

```sql
WITH daily AS (
  SELECT
    target_date,
    COUNT(*) AS total_rows,
    COUNT_IF(effective_available_capacity_mw IS NULL) AS available_nulls,
    COUNT_IF(effective_operating_capacity_mw IS NULL) AS operating_nulls
  FROM japan_interconnector_effective_capacity_all
  WHERE target_date <= DATE '2026-07-26'
  GROUP BY target_date
)
SELECT
  CAST(MIN(CASE WHEN operating_nulls > 0 THEN target_date END) AS varchar)
    AS repair_start_date,
  CAST(MAX(CASE WHEN operating_nulls > 0 THEN target_date END) AS varchar)
    AS repair_end_date,
  SUM(CASE WHEN operating_nulls > 0 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    AS affected_dates,
  MIN(total_rows) AS minimum_daily_rows,
  MAX(total_rows) AS maximum_daily_rows
FROM daily;
```

Paste the single result row and confirm whether the Airflow DAG is visible.

```sql
  COUNT(*) AS total_rows,
  COUNT_IF(effective_available_capacity_mw IS NULL) AS available_nulls,
  COUNT_IF(effective_operating_capacity_mw IS NULL) AS operating_nulls
FROM japan_interconnector_effective_capacity_all
WHERE target_date BETWEEN DATE '2019-01-31' AND DATE '2019-03-01';
```

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.095Z

Yes—dynamic task mapping is the right fix, and there’s likely an operational issue too.

The current design runs all ~93 chunks inside one 12-hour Python task. QA uses Celery workers with only 4 GiB RAM each, while task logs are neither persistent nor remotely stored. If that worker restarted, was OOM-killed, or stopped responding, the logs would disappear—matching what you observed. There is no intentional one-hour timeout in the DAG.

The improved one-run design should be:

- One planning task creates the object-aligned chunks.
- Mapped extraction/validation tasks process chunks independently, with concurrency limited to two.
- Each task writes compact evidence to the shared `/export` storage.
- One final task commits the prepared sources, reconciles, validates, and publishes.
- Failed chunks can retry independently.
- It remains one Airflow DAG run for you.

Before we patch, please check one thing:

1. Open the failed DAG run.
2. Click the red `run_scr_1207_backfill` task.
3. Open **Task Instance Details**.
4. Send me the task’s exact duration and any state reason/error shown—especially anything mentioning `worker lost`, `heartbeat`, `OOM`, `SIGTERM`, or “state changed externally.”

If there’s a **Download logs** option, try that too; don’t worry if it fails. I’d implement dynamic mapping regardless, but that detail tells us whether concurrency two is enough or whether the final publication task also needs tighter memory handling.

<skill>
<name>delegate-main-task</name>
<path>/Users/qn5792/.codex/skills/delegate-main-task/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: delegate-main-task
description: Delegate work to a separate, sidebar-visible Codex task in the same project, return immediately, and have that task message its result back to the origin. Use only when the user explicitly asks for a new, main, peer, or separate Codex task that reports back, or explicitly invokes $delegate-main-task. Do not use for ordinary subagent requests, unsolicited delegation, monitoring an existing task, or forking.
---

# Delegate Main Task

Create a normal peer task in the origin's current project. Let the origin finish its dispatch turn
immediately; have the peer start a new origin turn when its result is ready.

## Rules

- Treat `$delegate-main-task` or an explicit request for a new/separate Codex task as
  authorization to create it.
- Use Codex task tools, loading them with tool search when necessary.
- Require both `create_thread` and `send_message_to_thread` to be callable. If either is
  unavailable, explain that event-based delegation is unavailable and do not imitate it with
  polling.
- Use `create_thread`; never use `fork_thread`, subagent tools, worktrees, or cloud tasks.
- Create the task in the same saved project as the origin with
  `target.environment.type: local`.
- If the origin is projectless, create a projectless task.
- Preserve the user's scope, permissions, constraints, and completion criteria.
- Do not set a model or reasoning effort unless the user explicitly requests it.
- Do not archive the delegated task.

## Delegate

1. Resolve the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId` from app context or `list_threads`.
   Identify its project and use `list_projects` to resolve the exact `projectId`. Never guess
   between plausible tasks or projects.
2. Generate a short unique delegation ID.
3. Write a self-contained worker prompt containing:
   - The requested work, relevant context and paths, constraints, and expected evidence.
   - The delegation ID plus the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId`.
   - An instruction to call `send_message_to_thread` on the origin when the work reaches a
     terminal state. Send a concise, self-contained message containing the delegation ID,
     `completed`, `blocked`, or `failed`, the result, artifact paths or links, and the recommended
     next action.
   - An instruction to send `needs_input` with the exact question as soon as new user input or
     authority is required.
   - An instruction to keep ordinary progress in the worker task and send exactly one terminal
     result message to the origin.
4. Call `create_thread` once:
   - Saved project:
     `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`
   - Projectless origin: `target: {type: "projectless"}`
5. Retain the returned `threadId` and `hostId`.
6. End the origin's dispatch turn immediately. Tell the user the task is running and will message
   this task when ready. Do not call `wait_threads` or repeatedly read the worker.
7. Emit `::created-thread{threadId="THREAD_ID"}` on its own line in the origin's final response.

## Handle Returned Messages

- Treat a message carrying the delegation ID as a result from the existing delegation, not as a
  request to create another task.
- On `needs_input`, surface the exact question. After the user answers, send the answer to the
  worker with `send_message_to_thread`; then finish the origin turn again.
- On `completed`, integrate the returned result and continue the parent objective if work remains.
- Report failures or incomplete work plainly.

</skill>

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

<skill>
<name>delegate-main-task</name>
<path>/Users/qn5792/.codex/skills/delegate-main-task/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: delegate-main-task
description: Delegate work to a separate, sidebar-visible Codex task in the same project, return immediately, and have that task message its result back to the origin. Use only when the user explicitly asks for a new, main, peer, or separate Codex task that reports back, or explicitly invokes $delegate-main-task. Do not use for ordinary subagent requests, unsolicited delegation, monitoring an existing task, or forking.
---

# Delegate Main Task

Create a normal peer task in the origin's current project. Let the origin finish its dispatch turn
immediately; have the peer start a new origin turn when its result is ready.

## Rules

- Treat `$delegate-main-task` or an explicit request for a new/separate Codex task as
  authorization to create it.
- Use Codex task tools, loading them with tool search when necessary.
- Require both `create_thread` and `send_message_to_thread` to be callable. If either is
  unavailable, explain that event-based delegation is unavailable and do not imitate it with
  polling.
- Use `create_thread`; never use `fork_thread`, subagent tools, worktrees, or cloud tasks.
- Create the task in the same saved project as the origin with
  `target.environment.type: local`.
- If the origin is projectless, create a projectless task.
- Preserve the user's scope, permissions, constraints, and completion criteria.
- Do not set a model or reasoning effort unless the user explicitly requests it.
- Do not archive the delegated task.

## Delegate

1. Resolve the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId` from app context or `list_threads`.
   Identify its project and use `list_projects` to resolve the exact `projectId`. Never guess
   between plausible tasks or projects.
2. Generate a short unique delegation ID.
3. Write a self-contained worker prompt containing:
   - The requested work, relevant context and paths, constraints, and expected evidence.
   - The delegation ID plus the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId`.
   - An instruction to call `send_message_to_thread` on the origin when the work reaches a
     terminal state. Send a concise, self-contained message containing the delegation ID,
     `completed`, `blocked`, or `failed`, the result, artifact paths or links, and the recommended
     next action.
   - An instruction to send `needs_input` with the exact question as soon as new user input or
     authority is required.
   - An instruction to keep ordinary progress in the worker task and send exactly one terminal
     result message to the origin.
4. Call `create_thread` once:
   - Saved project:
     `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`
   - Projectless origin: `target: {type: "projectless"}`
5. Retain the returned `threadId` and `hostId`.
6. End the origin's dispatch turn immediately. Tell the user the task is running and will message
   this task when ready. Do not call `wait_threads` or repeatedly read the worker.
7. Emit `::created-thread{threadId="THREAD_ID"}` on its own line in the origin's final response.

## Handle Returned Messages

- Treat a message carrying the delegation ID as a result from the existing delegation, not as a
  request to create another task.
- On `needs_input`, surface the exact question. After the user answers, send the answer to the
  worker with `send_message_to_thread`; then finish the origin turn again.
- On `completed`, integrate the returned result and continue the parent objective if work remains.
- Report failures or incomplete work plainly.

</skill>

<skill>
<name>delegate-main-task</name>
<path>/Users/qn5792/.codex/skills/delegate-main-task/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: delegate-main-task
description: Delegate work to a separate, sidebar-visible Codex task in the same project, return immediately, and have that task message its result back to the origin. Use only when the user explicitly asks for a new, main, peer, or separate Codex task that reports back, or explicitly invokes $delegate-main-task. Do not use for ordinary subagent requests, unsolicited delegation, monitoring an existing task, or forking.
---

# Delegate Main Task

Create a normal peer task in the origin's current project. Let the origin finish its dispatch turn
immediately; have the peer start a new origin turn when its result is ready.

## Rules

- Treat `$delegate-main-task` or an explicit request for a new/separate Codex task as
  authorization to create it.
- Use Codex task tools, loading them with tool search when necessary.
- Require both `create_thread` and `send_message_to_thread` to be callable. If either is
  unavailable, explain that event-based delegation is unavailable and do not imitate it with
  polling.
- Use `create_thread`; never use `fork_thread`, subagent tools, worktrees, or cloud tasks.
- Create the task in the same saved project as the origin with
  `target.environment.type: local`.
- If the origin is projectless, create a projectless task.
- Preserve the user's scope, permissions, constraints, and completion criteria.
- Do not set a model or reasoning effort unless the user explicitly requests it.
- Do not archive the delegated task.

## Delegate

1. Resolve the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId` from app context or `list_threads`.
   Identify its project and use `list_projects` to resolve the exact `projectId`. Never guess
   between plausible tasks or projects.
2. Generate a short unique delegation ID.
3. Write a self-contained worker prompt containing:
   - The requested work, relevant context and paths, constraints, and expected evidence.
   - The delegation ID plus the origin's exact `threadId` and `hostId`.
   - An instruction to call `send_message_to_thread` on the origin when the work reaches a
     terminal state. Send a concise, self-contained message containing the delegation ID,
     `completed`, `blocked`, or `failed`, the result, artifact paths or links, and the recommended
     next action.
   - An instruction to send `needs_input` with the exact question as soon as new user input or
     authority is required.
   - An instruction to keep ordinary progress in the worker task and send exactly one terminal
     result message to the origin.
4. Call `create_thread` once:
   - Saved project:
     `target: {type: "project", projectId: "…", environment: {type: "local"}}`
   - Projectless origin: `target: {type: "projectless"}`
5. Retain the returned `threadId` and `hostId`.
6. End the origin's dispatch turn immediately. Tell the user the task is running and will message
   this task when ready. Do not call `wait_threads` or repeatedly read the worker.
7. Emit `::created-thread{threadId="THREAD_ID"}` on its own line in the origin's final response.

## Handle Returned Messages

- Treat a message carrying the delegation ID as a result from the existing delegation, not as a
  request to create another task.
- On `needs_input`, surface the exact question. After the user answers, send the answer to the
  worker with `send_message_to_thread`; then finish the origin turn again.
- On `completed`, integrate the returned result and continue the parent objective if work remains.
- Report failures or incomplete work plainly.

</skill>

### User - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.109Z

backfill dag running as we speak. 

Why can't I trigger the yearly->daily alongside backfill?

### Assistant - 2026-07-31T01:18:56.109Z

Because they mutate the same source inventory and effective publication that the backfill is validating and eventually replacing.

While the mapped preparation tasks are read-only, the final backfill task will:

- Recheck source fingerprints
- Snapshot the current effective baseline
- Replace historical source objects
- Publish affected-year files and the shared publication manifest

Meanwhile, yearly→daily DAGs would also upload capacity sources and trigger reconciliation against that same manifest. Cross-DAG runs are not protected by the backfill’s `max_active_runs=1`.

Best case, the backfill detects the concurrent change and fails safely. Worse, two reconciliations race and the last publisher preserves an outdated view of the other run’s changes.

So let the backfill finish first, then run yearly → monthly → weekly → daily sequentially. That also preserves intended precedence: broad yearly forecasts first, most specific daily values last.
